[
Structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
are as in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
.

There is an equivalent description of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
that is worth mentioning. First, notice that, since
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
is a tangent category, by Corollary
4.14
, it admits the construction of linear connections. In particular, one can define the universal vertical linear connection
(\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathsf{VLC}},\hat{\mathcal{K}})
for the tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, where
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
is a pointwise display differential bundle in the Hom-tangent category

\displaystyle[\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\>|\>[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]]

Then, we can construct the tangent category
\mathsf{LS}(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}})
of linear sections of the pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
. Such a tangent category coincides with the tangent category
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
.

To characterize the universal property enjoyed by
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
, consider a lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle(H,\gamma)

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})

By precomposing
(\mathbf{q}_{\mathsf{U}},\mathcal{S})
by
(H,\gamma)
we define an object
(\mathsf{U}\circ(H,\gamma);\mathcal{S}_{(H,\gamma)})
of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
. This extends to a functor:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})]\to\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})

Conversely, consider an object
(F,\alpha;\xi)
of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
. Define the following functor:

\displaystyle\Lambda[F,\alpha;\xi]\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})

which sends each object
X\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
to the pair
(FX,\xi_{X}\colon GFX\to KFX)
and each
f\colon X\to Y
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
to
Ff
. Furthermore, the distributive law
\alpha
of
(F,\alpha)
lifts to a distributive law for
\Lambda[F,\alpha;\xi]
. We claim that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\Lambda\colon\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})]
are inverse to each other.

Theorem 5.34
.

Consider a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
on the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
whose underlying tangent morphisms
(G,\beta)
and
(K,\theta)
are strong. The section
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathbf{q}_{\mathsf{U}}
is universal. Concretely, the induced strict natural transformation

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})]\to\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})

makes the functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}

which sends each tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
into a corepresentable functor. In particular,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}
is invertible.

Proof.

Consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
and a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
, which sends each
X\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
to the pair:

\displaystyle HX=(FX,\xi_{X}\colon GFX\to KFX)

We want to compare
(H,\gamma)
with
\Lambda[\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(H,\gamma)]
. Concretely,
\Lambda[\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(H,\gamma)]
sends an object
X
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
to the section
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(H,\gamma)_{X}
. However, this corresponds to the section
(FX,\xi_{X})
. Given a morphism
\varphi\colon(H,\gamma)\to(H^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
of
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q}))]

\displaystyle\varphi_{X}\colon(FX,\xi_{X}\colon GFX\to KFX)\to(F^{\prime}X,\xi^{\prime}_{X}\colon GF^{\prime}X\to KF^{\prime}X)

which coincides with the morphism
\Lambda[\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(\varphi)]
, since:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(\varphi)_{X}=FX\xrightarrow{\varphi_{X}}F^{\prime}X

Conversely, consider an object
(F,\alpha;\xi)
of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
. Therefore,
\Lambda[F,\alpha;\xi]
sends each
X
to
(FX,\xi_{X})
where
\xi_{X}\colon GFX\to KFX
. Therefore,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(\Lambda[F,\alpha;\xi])
coincides with
(F,\alpha;\xi)
. Similarly,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(\Lambda[\varphi])
coincides with
\varphi
, for each
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha;\xi)\to(F^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime};\xi^{\prime}))
.

Finally, it is not hard to see that the functor

\displaystyle\Lambda\colon\mathsf{LS}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})]

extends to a strict tangent morphism, since both
\Lambda[\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LS}}(F,\alpha;\xi)]
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\Lambda[F,\alpha;\xi])
send each
X\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
to the section

\displaystyle(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}X,\xi_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}})

Therefore,
\Lambda
inverts
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}
.
∎

Thanks to Theorem
5.34
, we can now introduce the construction of linear sections in the context of tangentads.

Definition 5.35
.

Consider a pair of tangentads
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
and a pointwise (display) differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. We say that
\mathbf{q}

admits the construction of linear sections
of
\mathbf{q}
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a tangent morphism
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and a section
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathbf{q}_{\mathsf{U}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}
is invertible. The section
(\mathsf{U},\mathcal{S})
is called the
universal linear section
of
\mathbf{q}
and
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
is called the
tangentad of linear sections
of
\mathbf{q}
.

Definition 5.36
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of linear sections
provided that every pointwise differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in every Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
admits the construction of linear sections.

We can now rephrase Theorem
5.34
as follows.

Corollary 5.37
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the constructions of linear sections and the tangentad of linear sections of a pointwise (display) differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
is the tangent category
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
.

Given a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
(whose projection is tangent display) in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
of lax tangent endo-morphisms over
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, there is always a canonical choice of pointwise (display) differential bundle, that is, the tangent bundle
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbf{p}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon(\mathrm{T},c)\to\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}
over the identity
\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}
. Thus, the tangentad
\mathsf{LS}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbf{p}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
of linear sections of
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbf{p}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
is precisely the tangentad of vector fields of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Theorem 5.38
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
.
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of vector fields if and only if the tangent bundle
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbf{p}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon(\mathrm{T},c)\to\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
admits the construction of linear sections.

We finally introduce the type of linear sections that we will use in the following section.

Definition 5.39
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of linear sections on differential bundles with a connection
when it admits the construction of linear connections and the pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{LC}}
of Proposition
5.8
admits the construction of linear sections. In the following, we denote by
\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
the tangentad
\mathsf{LS}(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}})
of linear sections of
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}
.

5.5
The formal structures of connections

In this section, we extend some constructions related to linear and affine connections. We start by showing that
\mathsf{VLC}
,
\mathsf{HLC}
,
\mathsf{LC}
,
\mathsf{VAC}
,
\mathsf{HAC}
, and
\mathsf{AC}
are
2
-functorial operations on the
2
-category of tangentads and strong tangent morphisms. Next, using the construction of linear sections on differential bundles with a connection, we introduce the constructions of the covariant derivative and the curvature tensor of linear connections. Finally, we discuss how to formalize the torsion tensor for affine connections.

The functoriality of the construction of connections

In this section, we show that the constructions of (vertical/horizontal) linear and affine connections are
2
-functorial in the
2
-category of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
.

Proposition 5.40
.

There are three
2
-functors

\displaystyle\mathsf{VLC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

\displaystyle\mathsf{HLC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

\displaystyle\mathsf{LC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

on the
2
-category of tangentads and display tangent morphisms, which send a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangent tangentads of (vertical/horizontal) linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

The proof is fairly similar to the one of PropositionsThe proof is fairly similar to the proofs of Proposition
3.13
, and
4.17
. Thus, we leave it to the reader to spell out the details.
∎

We obtain a similar result for affine connections.

Proposition 5.41
.

There are three
2
-endofunctors

\displaystyle\mathsf{VAC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

\displaystyle\mathsf{HAC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

\displaystyle\mathsf{AC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

on the
2
-category of tangentads and strong tangent morphisms, which send a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangent tangentads of (vertical/horizontal) affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

The covariant derivative of connections

Linear and affine connections in a tangent category come with a notion of covariant derivative. In this section, we show how to formalize this concept in the context of tangentads. Recall that the covariant derivative
\nabla^{k}
associated to a (vertical) linear connection
k
on a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
is an operation which takes a vector field
v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of the base object
M
of
\mathbf{q}
and a section
s\colon M\to E
of
\mathbf{q}
and yields a new section
\nabla^{k}_{v}s
of
\mathbf{q}
.

Therefore, while we consider only tangentads
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which admit the construction of (vertical) linear connections, we must also assume that
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of vector fields and the one of sections of differential bundles with a connection. In particular, let
\mathcal{K}
denotes the universal vertical connection of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
the underlying pointwise display differential bundle of the universal vertical connection,
\mathcal{V}
the universal vector field of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathcal{S}
the universal section of
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
.

We must also choose a vector field of the base of a linear connection with a connection, together with a section of the differential bundle. For this, we need to require the existence of the following
2
-pullback in
{\mathbf{K}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{LS}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{VF}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{VLC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VF}}}

(5.3)

Notice that, by
[
15
, Proposition 4.7]
, the diagram of Equation (
5.3
), becomes a
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

In the context of tangent categories, the objects of
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are fourth-tuples
(\mathbf{q},k;v,s)
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
, a vertical connection
k
on
\mathbf{q}
, a vector field
v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
on the base object of
\mathbf{q}
and a section
s\colon M\to E
of
\mathbf{q}
. A morphism

\displaystyle(f,g)

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbf{q},k;v,s)\to(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k^{\prime};v^{\prime},s^{\prime})

of
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a linear morphism of differential bundles
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
which commutes with the vertical connections, whose base morphism
f
commutes with the vector fields, that is,
v^{\prime}\circ f=\mathrm{T}f\circ v
, and such that
(f,g)
commutes with the sections, that is,
s^{\prime}\circ f=g\circ s
. Finally, the tangent structure of
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is computed as in
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

We can use the tangent morphisms

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathsf{VF}}}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathsf{LS}}}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}}\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

to pull back the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
, universal vertical linear connection
\mathcal{K}
, the underlying linear connection
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
, and the universal section
\mathcal{S}
to
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and obtain a vector field
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{\mathsf{VF}})
, a vertical linear connection
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{LS}})
, a linear connection
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{LS}})
, and a section
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(\pi_{\mathsf{LS}})
of
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
. Since
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
is a vertical linear connection on
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
, it defines a covariant derivative
\nabla^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}
. Thus, by applying
\nabla^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}
to the vector field
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}
and to the section
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
we obtain a new section
\nabla^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
to
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
.

However, since

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}=\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}_{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{LS}}}

is obtained by pre-composing
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
by
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{LS}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle(\mathsf{U}_{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{LS}},\nabla^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}})

defines an object of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}}(\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
. By the universal property of linear sections, the section
\nabla^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
corresponds to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\nabla\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Definition 5.42
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of the covariant derivative
when it admits the constructions of vertical linear connections, the construction of vector fields, the construction of linear sections of differential bundles with a connection, and the
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.3
) exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. Under these conditions, the
covariant derivative operator
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent morphism

\displaystyle\nabla\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which corresponds to the linear section
\nabla^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
.

Definition 5.43
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of the covariant derivative
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits such a construction.

Theorem 5.44
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the construction of the covariant derivative. Moreover, given a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, the corresponding covariant derivative operator is a functor which sends a fourth-tuple
(\mathbf{q},k;v,s)
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
, a vertical linear connection
k
of
\mathbf{q}
, a vector field
v
of
M
and a section
s
of
\mathbf{q}
, to the section
\nabla^{k}_{v}s
obtained by applying the covariant derivative of the vertical connection
k
to
v
and
s
.

The curvature of connections

In this section, we extend the notion of curvature tensor of linear connections to the formal context of tangentads. For starters, recall that the curvature tensor
\mathsf{R}
of a (vertical) linear connection
k
on a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is an operation which sends two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of the base object
M
of
\mathbf{q}
and a section
s\colon M\to E
of
\mathbf{q}
to a new section
\mathsf{R}^{k}_{u,v}s\colon M\to E
of
\mathbf{q}
.

This suggests considering a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the constructions of (vertical) linear connections, the construction of vector fields, and the construction of linear sections of differential bundles with a connection. Moreover, we shall assume the existence of the following trifold
2
-pullback in
{\mathbf{K}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VF}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Base}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VF}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

(5.4)

Notice that, by
[
15
, Proposition 4.7]
, the diagram of Equation (
5.4
), becomes a
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

In the context of tangent categories, the objects of
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are quintuples
(\mathbf{q},k;u,v,s)
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, a vertical linear connection
k
on
\mathbf{q}
, a pair of vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
on the base object
M
of
\mathbf{q}
, and a section
s\colon M\to E
of
\mathbf{q}
. A morphism from
(\mathbf{q},k;u,v,s)
to
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k^{\prime};u^{\prime},v^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is a linear morphism
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
of differential bundles which commute with the vertical connections
k
and
k^{\prime}
, whose base morphism
f\colon M\to M^{\prime}
commutes with the pair of vector fields
u
,
u^{\prime}
and
v
,
v^{\prime}
, that is,
v^{\prime}\circ f=\mathrm{T}f\circ v
and
u^{\prime}\circ f=\mathrm{T}f\circ u
, and such that
(f,g)
commutes with the sections
s
and
s^{\prime}
, that is,
s^{\prime}\circ f=g\circ s
. The tangent structure on
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
acts on the linear connection with connection
(\mathbf{q},k)
as in
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, on the vector fields
u
and
v
as in
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and on the section
s
as in
\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

We can use the tangent morphisms

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{3}}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}}\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

to pull back the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
, universal vertical linear connection
\mathcal{K}
, the underlying linear connection
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
, and the universal section
\mathcal{S}
to
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and obtain two vector fields
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{1})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{2})
, a vertical linear connection
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{3})
, a linear connection
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{3})
, and a section
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(\pi_{3})
of
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
.

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
is a vertical linear connection on
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
, it defines a curvature tensor
\mathsf{R}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}
. Thus, by applying
\mathsf{R}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}
to the vector fields
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}
and to the section
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
we obtain a new section
\mathsf{R}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
.

However, since

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\mathsf{VLC}}=\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}_{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{3}}

is obtained by pre-composing
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
by
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{3}
, it follows that

\displaystyle(\mathsf{U}_{\mathsf{LS}}\circ\pi_{3},\mathsf{R}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}})

defines an object of
\mathsf{LS}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}}(\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
. By the universal property of linear sections, the section
\mathsf{R}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
corresponds to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Definition 5.45
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of the curvature tensor
when it admits the constructions of vertical linear connections, the construction of vector fields, the construction of linear sections of differential bundles with a connection, and the trifold
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.4
) exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Under these conditions, the
curvature tensor operator
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent morphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{LSC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which corresponds to the linear section
\mathsf{R}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
.

Definition 5.46
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of the curvature tensor
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits such a construction.

Theorem 5.47
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the construction of the curvature tensor. Moreover, given a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, the corresponding curvature tensor operator is a functor which sends a tuple
(\mathbf{q},k;u,v,s)
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
, a vertical linear connection
k
of
\mathbf{q}
, a pair of vector fields
u
and
v
of
M
and a section
s
of
\mathbf{q}
, to the section
\mathsf{R}^{k}_{u,v}s
obtained by applying the curvature tensor of the vertical connection
k
to
u
,
v
, and
s
.

The torsion of connections

In this section, we extend the notion of torsion tensor of affine connections to the formal context of tangentads. For starters, recall that the torsion tensor
\mathsf{W}
of a (vertical) affine connection
k
on an object
M
of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is an operation which sends two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
to a new vector field
\mathsf{W}^{k}_{u}v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
. This suggests considering a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the constructions of (vertical) linear connections, and the construction of vector fields. Moreover, we shall assume the existence of the following
2
-pullback in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VF}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VF}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

(5.5)

Notice that, by
[
15
, Proposition 4.7]
, the diagram of Equation (
5.5
), becomes a
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

In the context of tangent categories, the objects of
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are fourth-tuples
(M,k;u,v)
formed by an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, a vertical affine connection
k
on
M
, and a pair of vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
on
M
. A morphism from
(\mathbf{q},k;u,v)
to
\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k^{\prime};u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
is a morphism
f\colon M\to M^{\prime}
which commutes with the vertical connections
k
and
k^{\prime}
and which commutes with the pair of vector fields
u
,
u^{\prime}
and
v
,
v^{\prime}
, that is,
v^{\prime}\circ f=\mathrm{T}f\circ v
and
u^{\prime}\circ f=\mathrm{T}f\circ u
. The tangent structure on
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
acts on the vertical affine connection
(M,k)
as in
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and on the vector fields
u
and
v
as in
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

We can use the tangent morphisms

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\pi_{3}}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

to pull back the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
, and universal vertical affine connection
\mathcal{K}
to
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and obtain two vector fields
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{1})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{2})
, and a vertical affine connection
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}(\pi_{3})
.

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
is a vertical affine connection, it defines a torsion tensor
\mathsf{W}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}
. Thus, by applying
\mathsf{W}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}
to the vector fields
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}
we obtain a new vector field
\mathsf{W}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}
.

However, by the universal property of vector fields, the vector field
\mathsf{W}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}
corresponds to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Definition 5.48
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of the torsion tensor
when it admits the constructions of vertical linear connections, the construction of vector fields, the construction of linear sections of differential bundles with a connection, and the
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.4
) exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Under these conditions, the
torsion tensor operator
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent morphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\times_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which corresponds to the vector field
\mathsf{W}^{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{1}}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{2}
.

Definition 5.49
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of the torsion tensor
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits such a construction.

Theorem 5.50
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the construction of the torsion tensor. Moreover, given a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, the corresponding torsion tensor operator is a functor which sends a tuple
(M,k;u,v)
formed by an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, a vertical affine connection
k
on
M
, and a pair of vector fields
u
and
v
of
M
to the vector field
\mathsf{W}^{k}_{u}v
obtained by applying the torsion tensor of the vertical connection
k
to
u
and
v
.

5.6
The construction of connections via (P)IE limits

PIE limits can be used to formally construct algebraic theories, such as the construction of algebras of a monad internal to a
2
-category. In
[
15
, Section 4.5]
, we used this property of PIE limits to construct the tangentads of vector fields of the tangentads of a
2
-category, by means of inserters and equifiers. The same cannot be done for the construction of differential objects and the constructions of differential bundles, since non-equational axioms (the universality of the differential projection of a differential object and the universality of the vertical lift of a display differential bundle, together with the existence of all pullbacks along the projection) are involved in the definitions of these two concepts.

The theory of affine connections is entirely algebraic, that is, it only involves equational axioms, and the only non-equational axioms demanded in the theory of linear connections are related to the differential bundles on which the connections are defined. In this section, we harness this property of connections and show how to construct the tangentads of (vertical/horizontal) affine and linear connections of the tangentads of a
2
-category via inserters and equifiers.

For starters, consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and let us construct the tangent categories of vertical, horizontal and linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Recall that a vertical connection on a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
is a morphism
k\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
and a horizontal connection on
\mathbf{q}
is a morphism
h\colon\mathsf{H}q=E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}E
. Consider the following two inserters:

\displaystyle(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})

\displaystyle\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}\Rightarrow\mathsf{Tot}

\displaystyle(\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{0})\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\Rightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}

where
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
is the universal pointwise display differential bundle of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q}=\mathsf{Tot}\times_{\mathsf{Base}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Base}
denotes the pointwise horizontal bundle of
\mathcal{Q}
, which is the tangent morphism which sends each differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
to
\mathsf{H}q=E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M\in(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Concretely,
(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})
is a tangent category equipped with a strict tangent morphism
V^{k}_{0}\colon(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a tangent
2
-morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{k}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{k}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Tot}}

and
(\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{0})
is a tangent category equipped with a strict tangent morphism
V^{h}_{0}\colon(\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{0})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a
2
-morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{h}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{h}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}}

The objects of
(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})
are pairs
(\mathbf{q},k_{0})
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
together with a morphism
k_{0}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
and morphisms
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q},k_{0})\to(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k_{0}^{\prime})
are morphisms
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
of differential bundles which commute with
k_{0}
and
k_{0}^{\prime}
, that is,
k_{0}^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{T}g=k_{0}\circ g
.

The objects of
(\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{0})
are pairs
(\mathbf{q},h_{0})
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
together with a morphism
h_{0}\colon\mathsf{H}q\to\mathrm{T}E
and morphisms
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q},h_{0})\to(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},h_{0}^{\prime})
are morphisms
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
of differential bundles which commute with
h_{0}
and
h_{0}^{\prime}
, that is,
h_{0}^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{T}g=h_{0}\circ(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)
.

So far, the morphisms
k_{0}
and
h_{0}
not need to satisfy any equational axioms. To impose the correct constraints, we use equifiers. For the sake of simplicity, we focus on vertical connections and leave it to the reader to replicate the same procedure for horizontal connections.

According to
[
4
, Lemma 3.3]
, a morphism
k\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
need to satisfy the following equations in order to define a vertical connection on
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&k\circ l_{q}=\mathsf{id}_{E}\\
&q\circ k=q\circ p_{E}\\
&l_{q}\circ k=\mathrm{T}k\circ l_{E}\\
&l_{q}\circ k=\mathrm{T}k\circ c_{E}\mathrm{T}l_{q}\end{split}

(5.6)

Let us start by imposing the equation
k\circ l_{q}=\mathsf{id}_{E}
. Consider the equifier:

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{1})\colon K_{0}\circ\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V^{k}_{0}}\to\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V^{k}_{0}}

Concretely,
(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{1})
is a tangent category equipped with a strict tangent morphism
W_{1}^{k}\colon(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{1})\to(\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{0})
satisfying the following equation:

\displaystyle(K_{0})_{W_{1}^{k}}\circ\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V^{k}_{0}\circ W_{1}^{k}}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V^{k}_{0}\circ W_{1}^{k}}

Thus,
(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{1})
is the tangent subcategory of
(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})
spanned by the objects
(\mathbf{q},k_{1})
, where
l_{q}\circ k_{1}=\mathsf{id}_{E}
. In the following, let us denote by
V_{1}^{k}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=W_{1}^{k}\circ V_{0}^{k}
and
K_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(K_{0})_{W_{1}^{k}}\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{1}^{k}\to\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{1}^{k}
. Next, we want to impose the equation
q\circ k=q\circ p
. Consider the equifier:

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{2})\colon\mathcal{Q}_{V_{1}^{k}}\circ K_{1}\to\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mup\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{1}^{k}}

(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{2})
is a tangent category equipped with a strict tangent morphism
W_{2}^{k}\colon(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{2})\to(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{1})
satisfying the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{V^{k}_{1}\circ W_{2}^{k}}\circ(K_{1})_{W_{2}^{k}}=\mathcal{Q}_{V^{k}_{1}\circ W_{2}^{k}}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mup\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{1}^{k}\circ W_{2}^{k}}

Let denote by
V_{2}^{k}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=W_{2}^{k}\circ V_{1}^{k}
and
K_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(K_{1})_{W_{2}^{k}}\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{2}^{k}\to\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{2}^{k}
. To impose the equation
l_{q}\circ k=\mathrm{T}k\circ l
, consider the equifier:

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{3},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{3})\colon\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V_{2}^{k}}\circ K_{2}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5muK_{2}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mul\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{2}^{k}}

(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{3},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{3})
is a tangent category equipped with a strict tangent morphism
W_{3}^{k}\colon(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{3},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{3})\to(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{2})
satisfying the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V_{2}^{k}\circ W_{3}^{k}}\circ(K_{2})_{W_{3}^{k}}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K_{2})_{W_{3}^{k}}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mul\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{2}^{k}\circ W_{3}^{k}}

Let denote by
V_{3}^{k}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=W_{3}^{k}\circ V_{2}^{k}
and
K_{3}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(K_{2})_{W_{3}^{k}}\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{3}^{k}\to\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{3}^{k}
. Finally, to impose the equation
l_{q}\circ k=\mathrm{T}k\circ c\circ\mathrm{T}l_{q}
, consider the equifier:

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{4},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4})\colon\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V_{3}^{k}}\circ K_{3}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5muK_{3}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muc\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{3}^{k}}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V_{3}^{k}}

(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{3},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{3})
is a tangent category equipped with a strict tangent morphism
W_{3}^{k}\colon(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{3},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{3})\to(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{2})
satisfying the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V_{3}^{k}\circ W_{4}^{k}}\circ(K_{3})_{W_{4}^{k}}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K_{3})_{W_{4}^{k}}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muc\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{3}^{k}\circ W_{4}^{k}}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{V_{3}^{k}\circ W_{4}^{k}}

(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{4},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4})
is precisely the tangent subcategory of
(\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})
spanned by those objects
(\mathbf{q},k)
whose morphisms
k\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
satisfy all the Equations (
5.6
).

Theorem 5.51
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the construction of display differential bundles and suppose that the inserter
\mathbb{I}^{k}_{0}
and the equifiers
\mathbb{E}^{k}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{E}^{k}_{4}
exist in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Thus,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of vertical linear connections in
{\mathbf{K}}
and the tangentad
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vertical linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the equifier
(\mathbb{E}^{k}_{4},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4})
.

Proof.

The proof is fairly similar to the proof of
[
15
, Theorem 4.8]
. The idea is to prove that for every tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
, the tangent category
\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\varphi_{4}^{k},\psi_{4}^{k})\cong[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4}]
, is isomorphic to the tangent category
\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
of vertical linear connections on the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, where
\varphi_{4}^{k}
and
\psi_{4}^{k}
denote the two
2
-morphisms equified by
(\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4})
. One starts by observing that an object of
\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\varphi_{4}^{k},\psi_{4}^{k})
consists of a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4})
such that
F_{\varphi_{4}^{k}}=F_{\psi_{4}^{k}}
. By postcomposing
(F,\alpha)
with

\displaystyle V_{4}^{k}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=W_{4}^{k}\circ V_{3}^{k}=W_{4}^{k}\circ{\dots}\circ W_{1}^{k}\circ V_{0}^{k}\colon(\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{4})\to(\mathbb{I}_{0}^{k},\mathbb{T}^{k}_{0})

we can equip
(F,\alpha)\circ V_{4}^{k}
with the tangent
2
-morphism
K_{4}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(K_{0})_{V_{4}^{k}}\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{4}^{k}\to\mathsf{Tot}\circ V_{4}^{k}
. Using the properties of the equifiers, we can show that
K_{4}
is a vertical linear connection on
(F,\alpha)\circ V_{4}^{k}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. Moreover, by the universal properties of the inserter
\mathbb{I}_{0}^{k}
and the equifiers
\mathbb{E}_{1}^{k},\mathbb{E}_{2}^{k},\mathbb{E}_{3}^{k}
, one can show that every vertical connection in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
corresponds to an object of
\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\varphi_{4}^{k},\psi_{4}^{k})
. This correspondence extends to an isomorphism of tangent categories:

\displaystyle\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\varphi_{4}^{k},\psi_{4}^{k})\cong\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

Finally, by using the universal property of the equifier
\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k}
, we obtain an isomorphism

\displaystyle[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k},\mathbb{T}_{4}^{k}]\cong\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\varphi_{4}^{k},\psi_{4}^{k})\cong\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

Thus,
(\mathbb{E}_{4}^{k},\mathbb{T}_{4}^{k})
is the tangentad of vertical linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

With a similar strategy, one can also construct horizontal linear connections with inserters and equifiers. Let denote by
(\mathbb{E}^{h}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{1}),\dots,(\mathbb{E}^{h}_{4},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{4})
the equifiers corresponding to the equations

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}q\circ h=\pi_{1}

\displaystyle p_{E}\circ h=\pi_{2}

\displaystyle l_{M}\circ h=\mathrm{T}h\circ(z_{E}\times_{z_{M}}l_{M})

\displaystyle c_{E}\circ\mathrm{T}l_{q}\circ h=\mathrm{T}h\circ(l_{q}\times_{z_{M}}z_{\mathrm{T}M})

respectively.

Theorem 5.52
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the construction of display differential bundles and suppose that the inserter
\mathbb{I}^{h}_{0}
and the equifiers
\mathbb{E}^{h}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{E}^{h}_{4}
exist in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Thus,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of horizontal linear connections in
{\mathbf{K}}
and the tangentad
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of horizontal linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the equifier
(\mathbb{E}^{h}_{4},\mathbb{T}^{h}_{4})
.

Proof.

The proof is substantially the same as the proof of Theorem
5.51
.
∎

To construct (full) linear connections, let us already assume that a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the constructions of vertical and horizontal linear connections and let us assume that the following
2
-pullback diagram exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{LC}_{0}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{HLC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{VLC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VLC}}}

(5.7)

In the context of tangent categories,
\mathsf{LC}_{0}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent category of triples
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
formed by a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}
, a vertical linear connection
k
of
\mathbf{q}
, and a horizontal linear connection
h
of
\mathbf{q}
. However, there is no required compatibility between
k
and
h
. To impose the right equations, once again, we turn to equifiers. We need to impose the equations

\displaystyle k\circ h=z_{q}\circ q\circ\pi_{2}

\displaystyle s_{E}\circ\left\langle\xi_{q}\circ\langle k,p_{M}\rangle,h\circ\langle p_{E},\mathrm{T}q\rangle\right\rangle=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}

by means of two equifiers that we denote by
(\mathbb{E}^{(k,h)}_{1},\mathbb{T}^{(k,h)}_{1})
and
(\mathbb{E}^{(k,h)}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{(k,h)}_{2})
, respectively.

Theorem 5.53
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
. Under the hypotheses of Theorems
5.51
and
5.52
and assuming the existence of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.7
) and of the equifiers
\mathbb{E}^{(k,h)}_{1}
and
\mathbb{E}^{(k,h)}_{2}
in
{\mathbf{K}}
,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of linear connections. Moreover, the tangentad
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the equifier
(\mathbb{E}^{(k,h)}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{(k,h)}_{2})
.

Corollary 5.54
.

If
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of differential bundles and it admits inserters and equifiers, then
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the constructions of vertical and horizontal linear connections. Moreover, if the
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.7
) is defined for each tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
, then
{\mathbf{K}}
also admits the construction of linear connections.

To construct (vertical/horizontal) affine connections via inserters and equifiers, one needs to follow the same procedures of Theorems
5.51
,
5.52
, and
5.53
, but replacing the universal pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
with the tangent bundle
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mup\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\to\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. In particular, the pullback of Equation (
5.7
) is replaced by the
2
-pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{AC}_{0}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{HAC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{VAC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}}

(5.8)

We denote by
({\mathbb{I}_{0}^{\prime}}^{k},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{0}}^{k})
,
({\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\prime}}^{k},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{k}),\dots,({\mathbb{E}_{4}^{\prime}}^{k},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{4}}^{k})
, and
({\mathbb{I}_{0}^{\prime}}^{h},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{0}}^{h})
,
({\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\prime}}^{h},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{h}),\dots,({\mathbb{E}_{4}^{\prime}}^{h},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{4}}^{h})
the corresponding inserters and equifiers to construct
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
({\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\prime}}^{(k,h)},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{(k,h)})
,
({\mathbb{E}_{2}^{\prime}}^{(k,h)},{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{2}}^{(k,h)})
the two remaining equifiers to construct
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Theorem 5.55
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
and assume that the inserter
\mathbb{I}_{0}^{\prime k}
and the equifiers
\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\prime k},\dots,E_{4}^{\prime k}
exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Thus,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of vertical affine connections in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Moreover, the tangentad
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vertical affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the equifier
(\mathbb{E}_{4}^{\prime k},\mathbb{T}_{4}^{\prime k})
.

Theorem 5.56
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
and assume that the inserter
\mathbb{I}_{0}^{\prime h}
and the equifiers
\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\prime h},\dots,\mathbb{E}_{4}^{\prime h}
exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Thus,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of horizontal affine connections in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Moreover, the tangentad
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of horizontal affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the equifier
(\mathbb{E}_{4}^{\prime h},\mathbb{T}_{4}^{\prime h})
.

Theorem 5.57
.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
. Under the assumptions of Theorems
5.55
and
5.56
and assuming the existence of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.8
) and of the equifiers
\mathbb{E}^{\prime(k,h)}_{1}
and
\mathbb{E}^{\prime(k,h)}_{2}
in
{\mathbf{K}}
,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of affine connections. Moreover, the tangentad
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the equifier
(\mathbb{E}^{\prime(k,h)}_{2},\mathbb{T}^{\prime(k,h)}_{2})
.

Corollary 5.58
.

If
{\mathbf{K}}
admits inserters and equifiers, then
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the constructions of vertical and horizontal affine connections. Moreover, if the
2
-pullback of Equation (
5.8
) is defined for each tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
, then
{\mathbf{K}}
also admits the construction of affine connections.

5.7
Applications

In Section
2.1
, we listed some examples of tangentads and in Sections
3.4
,
4.5
, and
4.5
we computed the constructions of vector fields, differential objects, and linear connections for tangent monads, tangent fibrations, tangent indexed categories, tangent split restriction categories, and showed how to extend these constructions to tangent restriction categories. In this section, we compute the constructions of linear and affine connections for these examples and show how to extend them to tangent restriction categories. Since the proofs are fairly similar to those for vector fields, we provide the final constructions without giving the details of the proofs. We also focus on (full) linear connections, since the construction of affine connections is fairly similar.

Tangent monads

Let us start by considering tangent monads. We previously discussed that strong tangent morphisms lift to the tangent categories of linear connections. It is not hard to convince ourselves that every strong tangent monad
(S,\alpha)
on a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, that is, a tangent monad whose underlying tangent morphism is strong, lifts to
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
as the tangent monad
\mathsf{LC}(S,\alpha)
.

Theorem 5.59
.

In the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGMND}
of tangent monads, each strong tangent monad
(S,\alpha)
on a tangent category admits the construction of linear connections. Moreover, the tangentads of linear connections of
(S,\alpha)
is the tangent monad
\mathsf{LC}(S,\alpha)
.

Tangent fibrations

Since the underlying functor
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a tangent fibration is a strict tangent morphism, hence strong, it lifts to a strict tangent morphism
\mathsf{LC}(\Pi)\colon\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. However, since
\Pi
is a tangent fibration, one can show that
\mathsf{LC}(\Pi)
is also a tangent fibration.

Lemma 5.60
.

Consider a (cloven) tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
between two tangent categories. The strict tangent morphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{LC}(\Pi)

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which sends a linear connection
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime};k^{\prime},h^{\prime})
to linear connection
(\Pi(\mathbf{q}^{\prime});\Pi(k^{\prime}),\Pi(h^{\prime}))
, is a (cloven) tangent fibration.

Theorem 5.61
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{FIB}
of (cloven) fibrations admits the constructions of linear connections. In particular, the tangentad of linear connections of a (cloven) tangent fibration
\Pi
is the tangent fibration
\mathsf{LC}(\Pi)
.

Tangent indexed categories

In this section, we employ the Grothendieck
2
-equivalence
\mathsf{TNGFIB}\simeq\mathsf{TNGINDX}
to compute the tangentad of linear connections for tangent indexed categories.

Proposition 5.62
.

Let
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
be two
2
-categories and suppose there is a
2
-equivalence
\Xi\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\simeq\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime})
of the
2
-categories of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
. If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of linear connections and
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
denotes the tangentad of linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, so does
\Xi(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and the tangentad of linear connections of
\Xi(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is
\Xi(\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
.

Proposition
5.62
together with the Grothendieck
2
-equivalence
\mathsf{TNGFIB}\cong\mathsf{TNGINDX}
allows us to compute the constructions of linear connections of tangent indexed categories. Consider a tangent indexed category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
whose underlying indexed category
\mathscr{I}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{CAT}
sends an object
M
of
\mathbb{X}
to the category
\mathbb{X}^{M}
and a morphism
f\colon M\to N
to the functor
f^{\ast}\colon\mathbb{X}^{N}\to\mathbb{X}^{M}
. Define
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
as follows:

​
[
Base tangent category
]
:

The base tangent category is
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
;

​
[
Indexed category
]
:

The indexed category
\mathsf{LC}(\mathscr{I})\colon\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{CAT}
sends a linear connection
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the category
\mathsf{LC}^{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}
whose objects are tuples
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime};k^{\prime},h^{\prime})
formed by a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{DB}^{\mathbf{q}}
and by two morphisms:

\displaystyle k^{\prime}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\to k^{\ast}E^{\prime}

\displaystyle h^{\prime}

\displaystyle\colon{\pi_{1}^{E}}^{\ast}E^{\prime}\times_{(\pi_{1}\circ q)^{\ast}M^{\prime}}{\pi_{2}^{\mathrm{T}M}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}M^{\prime}\to h^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}

such that
(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}^{\prime};k,k^{\prime};h,h^{\prime})
form a linear connection in the tangent category of elements of
\mathscr{I}
. Morphisms of
\mathsf{LC}^{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}
are morphisms
(\varphi,\psi)\colon\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathsf{DB}^{\mathbf{q}}
which commute with the connections.

Furthermore,
\mathsf{LC}(\mathscr{I})
sends a morphism
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q}_{1};k_{1},h_{1})\to(\mathbf{q}_{2};k_{2},h_{2})
of linear connections to the functor

\displaystyle(f,g)^{\ast}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{LC}^{(\mathbf{q}_{1};k_{1},h_{1})}\to\mathsf{LC}^{(\mathbf{q}_{2};k_{2},h_{2})}

which sends a tuple
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime};k^{\prime},h^{\prime})

, where
(f,g)^{\ast}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}=(\mathsf{DB}(\mathscr{I})(f,g))(\mathbf{q})
and:

\displaystyle(f,g)^{\ast}k^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{T}^{E_{1}}g^{\ast}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\xi^{g}}(\mathrm{T}g)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{E_{2}}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{T}g)^{\ast}k^{\prime}}(\mathrm{T}g)^{\ast}k_{2}^{\ast}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{k_{2}\circ\mathrm{T}g=g\circ k_{1}}k_{1}^{\ast}g^{\ast}E^{\prime}

\displaystyle(f,g)^{\ast}h^{\prime}\colon{\pi_{1}^{E_{1}}}^{\ast}g^{\ast}E^{\prime}\times_{(\pi_{1}^{E_{1}}\circ q_{1})^{\ast}f^{\ast}M^{\prime}}{\pi_{2}^{\mathrm{T}M_{1}}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{M_{1}}f^{\ast}M^{\prime}\cong

\displaystyle\qquad\cong(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}{\pi_{1}^{E_{2}}}^{\ast}E^{\prime}\times_{(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}(\pi_{1}^{E_{2}}\circ q_{2})M^{\prime}}(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}{\pi_{2}^{\mathrm{T}M_{2}}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{M_{2}}M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}h^{\prime}}(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}h^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{E_{2}}E^{\prime}\cong

\displaystyle\qquad\cong h^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}g)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{E_{2}}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\xi^{g}}h^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{E_{1}}g^{\ast}E^{\prime}

where we used the following equalities:

\displaystyle g\circ\pi_{1}^{E_{1}}=\pi_{1}^{E_{2}}\circ(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)

\displaystyle g\circ\pi_{2}^{E_{1}}=\pi_{2}^{E_{2}}\circ(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)

\displaystyle f\circ q_{1}\circ\pi_{1}^{E_{1}}=q_{2}\circ\pi_{1}^{E_{2}}\circ(g\times_{f}\mathrm{T}f)

[
Indexed tangent bundle functor
]
:

The indexed tangent bundle functor
\mathsf{LC}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime})
consists of the list of functors

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{\prime(\mathbf{q};k,h)}\colon\mathsf{LC}^{\mathbf{q}}\to\mathsf{LC}^{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LC}}(\mathbf{q};k,h)}

which send a tuple
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime};k^{\prime},h^{\prime})
to the tuple
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}^{\prime};\mathrm{T}^{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}k^{\prime},\mathrm{T}^{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}h^{\prime})
where:

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}k^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{T}E}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{c^{\prime}}c^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{T}E}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}k^{\prime}}c^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}k^{\ast}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\xi_{k}}c^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}k)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathscr{I}_{2}}k_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}h^{\prime}\colon\pi_{1}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\times_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}M^{\prime}}\pi_{2}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{id}\times c^{\prime}}(\mathrm{T}\pi_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\times_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}M^{\prime}}\pi_{2}^{\ast}c^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}M^{\prime}\cong

\displaystyle\qquad\cong(\mathsf{id}_{E}\times_{M}c)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M}(\pi_{1}^{\ast}E^{\prime}\times_{M^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}M^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(\mathsf{id}_{E}\times_{M}c)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M}h^{\prime}}(\mathsf{id}_{E}\times_{M}c)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M}h^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\relbar\mkern-5.0mu\relbar\mkern-5.0mu\relbar

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{\xi_{h}}(\mathsf{id}_{E}\times_{M}c)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}h)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}E}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{(\mathsf{id}_{E}\times_{M}c)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}h)^{\ast}c^{\prime}}(\mathsf{id}_{E}\times_{M}c)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}h)^{\ast}c^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}E}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathscr{I}_{2}}h_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}E}\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}E^{\prime}

Moreover,
\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathbf{q}}
sends a morphism
(\varphi,\psi)\colon(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}_{1};k^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{1})\to(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}_{2};k^{\prime}_{2},h^{\prime}_{2})
to
(\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}\varphi,\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}\psi)
. The distributors of
\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathbf{q}}
are pairs
(\xi^{f},\xi^{g})
of distributors of
\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime E}
;

​
[
Indexed natural transformations
]
:

The structural indexed natural transformations of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathscr{I})
are the same as for
\mathscr{I}
.

Theorem 5.63
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{INDX}
of indexed categories admits the construction of linear connections. In particular, given a tangent indexed category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, the tangent indexed category of linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
is the tangent indexed category
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})=(\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}),\mathsf{LC}(\mathscr{I}),\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime}))
.

Tangent split restriction categories

In this section, we consider the construction of linear connections for tangent split restriction categories.

Lemma 5.64
.

Consider a tangent split restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and let
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be the category whose objects are linear connections
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
in the tangent subcategory
\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and morphisms are morphisms
(f,g)
of linear connections such that also their restriction idempotents
(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mug\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
are also morphisms of linear connections. The tangent split restriction structure on
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
lifts to
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 5.65
.

A
restriction differential bundle
in a Cartesian tangent split restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is an object of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Concretely, a restriction differential bundle consists of a restriction differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
together with two total maps

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\colon E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}E

where
E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M
is the restriction pullback of
q
along
p_{M}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

The total maps
k
and
h
satisfy the same equational axioms of a linear connection in ordinary tangent category theory.

Theorem 5.66
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{SRCAT}
of split restriction categories admits the construction of linear connections. In particular, the tangentad of linear connections of a Cartesian tangent split restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the Cartesian tangent split restriction category
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Tangent restriction categories: a general approach

To extend the construction of linear connections to tangent restriction categories, we adopt a similar technique to the one used to extend the constructions differential bundles.

Consider a pullback-extension context, that is, two
2
-categories
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{J}}
together with two
2
-functors

\displaystyle\Xi\colon{\mathbf{J}}\leftrightarrows\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\colon\mathsf{Inc}

together with a natural
2
-transformation

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathbb{X}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathsf{Inc}(\Xi(\mathbb{X}))

natural in
\mathbb{X}\in{\mathbf{J}}
. Let us also consider an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{J}}
such that the tangentad
\Xi(\mathbb{X})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of linear connections. Finally, let us assume the existence of the following
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{Prll}_{2}({\mathbf{J}})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{LC}(\Xi(\mathbb{X}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Inc}(\Xi(\mathbb{X}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{LC}(\eta,\eta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{LC}}_{\mathbb{X}},\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}_{\mathbb{X}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{LC}}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}),\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\eta,\eta)}

(5.9)

Definition 5.67
.

Let
({\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{J}};\mathsf{Inc},\Xi;\eta)
be a pullback-extension context. An object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{J}}
admits the
extended construction of linear connections
(w.r.t. to the pullback-extension context) if
\Xi(\mathbb{X})
admits the construction of linear connections in
{\mathbf{K}}
, and the
2
-pullback diagram of Equation (
5.9
) exists in
\mathsf{Prll}_{2}({\mathbf{J}})
. In this scenario, the
object of linear connections
(w.r.t. to the pullback-extension context) of
\mathbb{X}
is the object
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X})
of
{\mathbf{J}}
, that is, the
2
-pullback of
(\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{LC}}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}),\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}))
along
(\eta,\eta)
.

Consider a generic tangent restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and let us unwrap the definition of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
. The objects of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
are tuples
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
formed by a restriction differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of
\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with two morphisms

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\colon E\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}E

such that:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muk\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ k=k

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muh\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\times_{M}\mathrm{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muz\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{q}

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ h=h

Moreover,
k
and
h
satisfy the axioms of a restriction differential bundle.

A morphism
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q}_{1};k_{1},h_{1})\to(\mathbf{q}_{2};k_{2},h_{2})
of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
consists of a morphism
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}_{1}\to\mathbf{q}_{2}
of restriction differential bundles which commutes with the structural morphism of the linear connections and with their restriction idempotents.

Now, consider a tangent (non-necessarily split) restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and define
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to be the full subcategory of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
spanned by the objects
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
where
q
,
z_{q}
,
s_{q}
,
l_{q}
,
k
, and
h
are total maps in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, which is precisely when
q
and
z_{q}
are total.

Lemma 5.68
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be a tangent restriction category. The subcategory
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{LC}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
spanned by the objects
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
where
q
and
z_{q}
are total in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a tangent restriction category.

We can finally prove the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 5.69
.

Consider the pullback-extension context
(\mathsf{RCAT},\mathsf{TNGRCAT};\mathsf{Inc},\mathsf{Split}_{R};\eta)
of tangent restriction categories. The
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGRCAT}
admits the extended construction of linear connections with respect to this pullback-extension context. Moreover, the object of linear connections of a tangent restriction category is the tangent restriction category
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
